2026-11-16T15:58:37.6264850Z [36;1m# Environment Variables[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6265187Z [36;1m# Config File[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6265469Z [36;1m# Defaults[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6265731Z [36;1m#[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6266232Z [36;1m# As noted above defaults are awkward to handle as GitHub Actions will inject those values as the input[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6267144Z [36;1m# if the caller doesn't provide them, thus if the input matches the default we don't set it as we[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6268025Z [36;1m# can't tell the difference.  Plus if we did set it when it was the default value then it could potentially[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6268994Z [36;1m# override an external environment variable, or something in the callers configuration file, which then wouldn't[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6269791Z [36;1m# match the configuration priority that is documented.[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6270269Z [36;1mset_env_var_if_provided() {[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6270637Z [36;1m  local var_name="$1"[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6270993Z [36;1m  local input_value="$2"[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6271364Z [36;1m  local default_value="$3"[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6271683Z [36;1m[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6272040Z [36;1m  if [ -n "$input_value" ] && [ "$input_value" != "$default_value" ]; then[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6272613Z [36;1m    # If action was provided with explicit input by the caller set that[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6273379Z [36;1m    # Use printf %q to safely escape special characters and prevent command injection[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6274037Z [36;1m    printf 'export %s=%q\n' "$var_name" "$input_value" >> trivy_envs.txt[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6274542Z [36;1m  fi[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6274819Z [36;1m}[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6275076Z [36;1m[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6275480Z [36;1m# Set environment variables, handling those with default values[0m
2026-11-16T15:58:37.6276264Z [36;1m# cf. https://aquasecurity.github.io/trivy/latest/docs/configuration/#environment-variables[0m

2026-11-16T15:59:54.6781184Z ##[start-action display=Set Trivy environment variables;id=__aquasecurity_trivy-action_2.__run_3]
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7833456Z ##[group]Run # Note: There is currently no way to distinguish between undefined variables and empty strings in GitHub Actions.
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7834629Z [36;1m# Note: There is currently no way to distinguish between undefined variables and empty strings in GitHub Actions.[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7835535Z [36;1m# This limitation affects how we handle default values and empty inputs.[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7836298Z [36;1m# For more information, see: https://github.com/actions/runner/issues/924[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7836842Z [36;1m[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7837365Z [36;1m# The following logic implements the configuration priority described in the README:[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7837989Z [36;1m#[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7838271Z [36;1m# Inputs[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7838588Z [36;1m# Environment Variables[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7838969Z [36;1m# Config File[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7839295Z [36;1m# Defaults[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7839603Z [36;1m#[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7840148Z [36;1m# As noted above defaults are awkward to handle as GitHub Actions will inject those values as the input[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7840953Z [36;1m# if the caller doesn't provide them, thus if the input matches the default we don't set it as we[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7841748Z [36;1m# can't tell the difference.  Plus if we did set it when it was the default value then it could potentially[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7842922Z [36;1m# override an external environment variable, or something in the callers configuration file, which then wouldn't[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7843870Z [36;1m# match the configuration priority that is documented.[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7844364Z [36;1mset_env_var_if_provided() {[0m
2026-11-16T15:59:54.7844766Z [36;1m  local var_name="$1"[0m
